1	# Chores-07.md
2	
3	General chores notes — design captures (forward-looking) and
4	post-implementation chore entries. Same shape as chores-01..06.md;
5	07 starts here only because 06 grew large during the 0.40.0 /
6	0.41.0 cycles and a new top-level cycle is a natural file
7	boundary.
8	
9	Subsection headers use the trailing-version format from CLAUDE.md
10	when they correspond to a release: `## Description (X.Y.Z)`.
11	Pre-implementation design captures may use a plain title; once
12	implemented, the title can become a release-versioned chore.
13	
14	## init + clone redesign (0.41.1)
15	
16	Empirical validation 2026-04-27 against `vc-x1 0.42.0-3` (see
17	`notes/vc-x1-init.md`) surfaced cosmetic anomalies and a
18	substantive design gap. Init's flag surface (`--repo-local`,
19	`--repo-remote`, `--owner`, `--dir`, `[NAME]`) carries 6+
20	mutually-exclusive preflight checks just to prevent impossible
21	combinations from being typed. Clone already implements a
22	unified-positional alternative (URL / `owner/name` shorthand);
23	this cycle extends that pattern to init, adds POR support to
24	both, and unifies their CLI surfaces.
25	
26	Branched off 0.41.0 (commit `6747a27`). On close-out, the
27	in-flight 0.42.0 work rebases on top.
28	
29	### Command structure
30	
31	```
32	vc-x1 init  <TARGET> [NAME] [--scope code,bot|por] [--private] [--dry-run]
33	vc-x1 clone <TARGET> [NAME] [--scope code,bot|por]             [--dry-run]
34	```
35	
36	Identical surfaces modulo `--private` (init only).
37	
38	`<TARGET>` accepts:
39	
40	- **URL** — `git@host:owner/name(.git)?`, `https://...(.git)?`
41	  (detected by `://` or SSH `<host>:<path>` shape with `@`).
42	- **`owner/name` shorthand** — single `/`, no path prefix;
43	  resolved to `git@github.com:owner/name.git`.
44	- **Path-prefixed** — `./X`, `../X`, `/X`, `~/X`, `~`. Path
45	  IS the target; last component is the workspace name. Bare
46	  `.` and bare `NAME` are errors (require explicit prefix).
47	
48	`[NAME]`:
49	
50	- For URL / `owner/name` forms: overrides the derived
51	  destination dir name. `vc-x1 clone owner/foo my-name`
52	  clones into `./my-name/` instead of `./foo/`.
53	- For path-prefix form: error if combined (path already
54	  specifies the full target).
55	- Path-form for clone: error (clone needs a remote URL;
56	  see "Operations" for clone's target rules).
57	
58	`--scope`:
59	
60	- `code,bot` (default): both repos, dual-repo layout.
61	- `por`: single repo, no `.vc-config.toml` written.
62	- `code` / `bot` standalone: error. Reason — these are
63	  config-lookup keywords; init has no config to look up
64	  against, and clone's simplification omits them too. The
65	  manual decomposition (clone code as POR, clone bot as
66	  POR, place at `.claude/`, run `vc-x1 symlink`) covers
67	  the rare cases where a user wants the dual layout
68	  composed by hand.
69	
70	`--private`: only meaningful for init when creating a new
71	GitHub repo. Sets visibility at `gh repo create`. Silently
72	warn-and-ignore if the remote already exists. Errors on
73	clone (clone never creates a remote).
74	
75	`--dry-run`: validate the operation as far as possible
76	without side effects. Print what would be done.
77	
78	### Operations
79	
80	**Init** is *smart* — it detects the target dir's state and
81	chooses the right operation.
82	
83	| Target state | `--scope=code,bot` | `--scope=por` |
84	|---|---|---|
85	| empty / nonexistent | create dual-repo from scratch | create POR |
86	| POR (jj+git, no config) | upgrade to dual | error (already POR) |
87	| POR (git only, no `.jj/`) | bootstrap `jj git init`, then upgrade | error (already POR-shape) |
88	| code POR + `.claude/` peer POR (configs missing) | write configs only | error |
89	| dual-repo (configs present) | error (already there) | error (downgrade) |
90	| single-repo (illegal shape) | error | error |
91	| anything else (random files, etc.) | error with diagnostic | error with diagnostic |
92	
93	**Clone** is *dumb* — it just clones URLs into target dirs.
94	
95	| `--scope=por` | clone the URL into `<target>` |
96	| `--scope=code,bot` | clone code URL into `<target>`, derive bot URL (`<URL>.claude`), clone into `<target>/.claude/`, then create symlink via `vc-x1 symlink` |
97	
98	For both clone modes: target must NOT exist (`vc-x1 clone`
99	errors if anything is there). Use `vc-x1 sync` to update an
100	existing checkout.
101	
102	`.vc-config.toml` files for the dual-repo case are *tracked
103	content in the remotes*, not files clone writes. They ride
104	along with the clone. If a remote doesn't have them
105	committed, `vc-x1 init <path> --scope=code,bot` from inside
106	the cloned dir handles the config-only upgrade case (see
107	init's state table above).
108	
109	`--scope=code,bot` is implemented as in-process composition
110	of the `--scope=por` primitive (called twice: once for code
111	URL, once for derived bot URL) plus a `symlink::create`
112	call. No subprocess overhead.
113	
114	If init or clone fails partway through, manual cleanup is
115	required (no rollback).
116	
117	### Example Layout local repos
118	
119	cwd = ~/prgs
120	
121	`vc-init test-repos/tf1 --scope=code,bot`
122	`vc-init test-repos/tf1`
123	
124	TODO: add actual output of ls or tree cmds (fill at close-out)
125	
126	`vc-init ../test-repos/tf1 --scope=code,bot`
127	`vc-init ../test-repos/tf1`
128	
129	TODO: add actual output of ls or tree cmds (fill at close-out)
130	
131	### Preflight
132	
133	- Verify `jj` is installed (`jj --version`); friendly error
134	  with install link if missing.
135	- For init URL / `owner/name` forms: probe with `git
136	  ls-remote <url>`. Exists → clone. Doesn't → create via
137	  `gh repo create` (errors if host isn't GitHub or `gh`
138	  missing).
139	- For clone URL / `owner/name` forms: probe with `git
140	  ls-remote <url>`. Exists → clone. Doesn't → error
141	  (clone is dumb, no auto-create).
142	
143	### Edits
144	
145	- `Cargo.toml`: bump to `0.41.1`.
146	- New `src/repo_url.rs` (or fold into `src/common.rs`): lift
147	  `derive_name` and `resolve_url` from `clone.rs`. Add
148	  `parse_target(s) -> Target` enum `{Url(String),
149	  OwnerName(String, String), Path(PathBuf)}`. Single source
150	  of truth for positional parsing across init and clone.
151	- `src/clone.rs`:
152	  - `CloneArgs`: `<TARGET>` + optional `[NAME]` positionals;
153	    add `--scope code,bot|por` (default `code,bot`); drop
154	    `--dir`. Old `--repo` positional rename: `repo` →
155	    `target`.
156	  - Refactor body into `clone_one(url, target_dir)` (the
157	    primitive, also used by `--scope=por`) and
158	    `clone_dual(code_url, target_dir)` (orchestrator —
159	    derives bot URL, calls `clone_one` twice, calls
160	    `symlink::create`).
161	  - Pre-clone check: error if `target_dir` exists.
162	- `src/init.rs`:
163	  - `InitArgs`: drop `owner`, `repo_local`, `repo_remote`,
164	    `dir`; replace bare-name `name` positional with
165	    `<TARGET>` + optional `[NAME]`.
166	  - Remove the 6 mutually-exclusive flag checks.
167	  - New `detect_state(path) -> InitTargetState`
168	    `{Empty, PorJjGit, PorGitOnly, PorWithPeerPor,
169	    SingleRepoWorkspace, DualRepoWorkspace, Other(reason)}`.
170	  - Refactor body into `init_one(target_dir, opts)` (the
171	    `--scope=por` primitive, also used as a step inside
172	    `--scope=code,bot`) and `init_dual(target_dir, opts)`
173	    (orchestrator — calls `init_one` twice, writes both
174	    `.vc-config.toml` files, ochid-links, `symlink::create`).
175	  - POR upgrade paths:
176	    - PorJjGit + `--scope=code,bot` → skip code-side init,
177	      run bot-side `init_one` into `.claude/`, write configs,
178	      ochid-link existing code commit + new bot commit,
179	      symlink.
180	    - PorGitOnly + `--scope=code,bot` → `jj git init` first,
181	      then PorJjGit path.
182	    - PorWithPeerPor + `--scope=code,bot` → write only the
183	      two `.vc-config.toml` files + symlink (both repos
184	      already exist).
185	- `src/test_helpers.rs`: `Fixture::new_opts` reshapes to use
186	  the new `target` positional shape (and `[NAME]` if needed).
187	- `notes/todo.md`: add this cycle to In Progress.
188	- `notes/chores-07.md`: per-step post-impl subsections.
189	- `notes/vc-x1-init.md`: close out cosmetic anomalies (fold
190	  into final close-out).
191	
192	### Cycle structure — multi-step
193	
194	- `-0` — plan + version bump + this notes section.
195	- `-1` — lift `derive_name` / `resolve_url` / `parse_target`
196	  to shared module; clone migrates internally (no behavior
197	  change yet).
198	- `-2` — clone reshape: `<TARGET>` + `[NAME]` positionals,
199	  add `--scope code,bot|por`, refactor into `clone_one` /
200	  `clone_dual`. Add target-exists pre-check.
201	- `-3` — init reshape: drop old flags, add `<TARGET>` +
202	  `[NAME]`, add `--scope=por`, refactor into `init_one` /
203	  `init_dual`. Existing create-from-empty operations work
204	  via the new shape.
205	- `-4` — init POR detection + upgrade paths
206	  (PorJjGit, PorGitOnly auto-bootstrap, PorWithPeerPor
207	  config-only).
208	- `-5` — `test_helpers::Fixture` migration; audit downstream
209	  callers across the test suite.
210	- final — cycle close-out: fill in Example Layout outputs,
211	  address `notes/vc-x1-init.md` cosmetic anomalies, drop
212	  the `-N` suffix.
213	
214	### Decisions made during design
215	
216	- **Version + cycle line.** This work + the sync `--check`
217	  fix land on the 0.41.x line. Init+clone = 0.41.1. Sync
218	  fix = a separate cycle (likely 0.41.2). Then rebase the
219	  in-flight 0.42.0 work on top of both.
220	- **Path-prefix vocabulary.** `./NAME` and the standard
221	  prefixes (`../`, `/`, `~/`, `~`) only. Bare `.` and bare
222	  `NAME` are errors — explicit prefix required.
223	- **`--private` on existing remote.** Warn and ignore;
224	  visibility was set at create time.
225	- **Cosmetic anomalies** from `notes/vc-x1-init.md` —
226	  addressed at close-out, not deferred.
227	- **`--scope=code` and `--scope=bot` for clone.** Dropped
228	  from the menu. Manual decomposition (two `--scope=por`
229	  clones + `vc-x1 symlink`) covers the use case.
230	- **Composition over duplication.** `--scope=code,bot` is
231	  implemented as in-process composition of the `--scope=por`
232	  primitive — single source of truth for the actual
233	  clone/init operation, thin wrapper for the dual case.

235	## Substep protocol formalization (0.42.0-4.5)
236	
237	Notes-only side cycle. Branched off `main` at 0.42.0-4
238	(init+clone redesign capture); landed between 0.42.0-4
239	and the planned 0.42.0-5 (finalize --scope). Formalizes
240	the substep protocol first exercised in 0.41.1-6.5,
241	validates the close-out squash recipe, and pulls the
242	supporting jj revset vocabulary into a sibling cheatsheet
243	so the protocol can refer to it without duplicating.
244	
245	Version path: numbered `0.42.0-4.5` rather than `0.42.1-0`
246	to avoid pre-empting the patch line while 0.42.0 is
247	mid-cycle. The `.5` suffix positions this as side work
248	between -4 and -5 — semver-legal (pre-release identifiers
249	are dot-separated numeric components and compare
250	numerically) and keeps Cargo.toml's version monotonically
251	increasing.
252	
253	The user opened the cycle by drafting `notes/jj-revsets.md`
254	from their own learning notes (using `notes/substep-test.sh`
255	to build a scratch ladder under `/tmp/substep-test`). The
256	bot reviewed, proposed the substep protocol formalization,
257	and the work landed in five substeps using the protocol it
258	was documenting — dogfood validation in flight.
259	
260	### Edits
261	
262	- `notes/jj-revsets.md`: new. Revset primitives cheatsheet
263	  (chid/cid stability, `@`/`@-`/`@+`, `..`/`::` ranges,
264	  prefix matching). User-authored intro and worked
265	  examples; bot-added `### Interpretation` blocks
266	  cataloguing the operator semantics from the empirical
267	  data, two review passes for typos and grammar.
268	- `notes/substep-protocol.md`: new. Formal protocol of
269	  record. Five sections — purpose, per-substep contract
270	  (`cargo test --bins` non-negotiable), navigation
271	  (cross-link to jj-revsets.md), close-out, recovery —
272	  plus a worked end-to-end example. Drops the prior
273	  "draft text / working hypothesis" hedges. Close-out
274	  recipe validated against scratch ladders at N=3 and N=5
275	  substeps:
276	  ```
277	  jj squash --from "<base>..@-" --into @ -u -R .
278	  ```
279	- `notes/substep-test.sh`: new. Four-revision ladder
280	  scaffold under `/tmp/substep-test`. Used to validate
281	  the squash recipe; remains as a reusable scratch tool
282	  for future substep experiments.
283	- `CLAUDE.md`: `#### Substeps within a multi-step X.Y.Z-N`
284	  subsection added under `### Versioning`. Points at
285	  `notes/substep-protocol.md` (full procedure) and
286	  `notes/jj-revsets.md` (revset primitives the procedure
287	  relies on). Conservative integration scope; broader
288	  CLAUDE.md reorg candidates (consolidating the
289	  `## Committing` / `## Commit Message Style` /
290	  `## ochid Trailers` triplet, surfacing recovery as its
291	  own section, top-of-file TOC) surfaced in the substep-4
292	  commit body for next-cycle discussion.
293	- `notes/todo.md`: `## Done` entry; reference [73] points
294	  here.
295	- `notes/chores-07.md`: this subsection.
296	
297	### Substep ladder (squashed at close-out)
298	
299	- substep 0: `notes/jj-revsets.md` review fixes (typos,
300	  two `### Interpretation` blocks for relative- and
301	  absolute-revset operators).
302	- substep 1: validate close-out squash recipe against
303	  N=3 and N=5 ladders in `/tmp/substep-test`. No file
304	  changes; finding folded into the next substep.
305	- substep 2: `notes/substep-protocol.md` formal rewrite.
306	- substep 3: `CLAUDE.md` pointer subsection.
307	- substep 4: second-pass review fixes after the user's
308	  in-place tweaks added the concrete `lu::` example and
309	  surfaced typos in the absolute-revsets bullet list.
310	- close-out: this commit. Single squashed commit on
311	  `main` via the validated recipe.
312	
313	### Decisions made during design
314	
315	- **`-R .` explicitness.** The protocol shows `-R .` on
316	  every `jj` invocation. For the bot in a dual-repo
317	  workspace this is correct (always be explicit about
318	  which repo); for human users in a single-repo project
319	  the flag is optional. Closing note in the protocol
320	  spells this out.
321	- **Linear vs parallel substep topology.** The ladder is
322	  linear (`jj new` chains commits). User noted at
323	  close-out that a parallel-then-merge topology
324	  (`jj new -r <chid>` to branch from a specific point)
325	  might give cleaner per-concern diffs. Captured as a
326	  future experiment; the close-out recipe would change
327	  too — `<base>..@-` assumes linear ancestry.
328	- **`jj op restore` as recovery vs close-out.** The draft
329	  protocol had floated `jj op restore` as a close-out
330	  alternative. Validation showed it discards substep
331	  work — it's a recovery tool, not close-out. Final
332	  protocol keeps it under Recovery only.
333	- **`-u` on the close-out squash.** `--use-destination-message`
334	  preserves `@`'s description through the squash (which
335	  `vc-x1 push`'s `commit-app` then overwrites with the
336	  close-out title/body anyway). Without `-u`, jj opens
337	  `$EDITOR` to combine source and destination
338	  descriptions — unwanted at close-out, since none of
339	  the substep titles are meant to ship.
340	
341	## init-clone-refactor recovery (0.42.0-4.6)
342	
343	Notes-only side cycle. Sibling to 0.42.0-4.5 — both
344	land between 0.42.0-4 and the planned 0.42.0-5 while
345	the main ladder is paused. Captures the 2026-05-02
346	recovery of the `init-clone-refactor` branch from a
347	destructive local rewrite and reframes
348	`notes/init-clone-refactor-conflict.md` from a
349	forward-looking brief into a post-mortem + reusable
350	playbook.
351	
352	### Background
353	
354	The branch had been broken for several days: 8 commits
355	in conflict state, branched from 0.41.0, never rebased
356	onto post-0.42.0 main. The original brief (drafted
357	2026-05-01) hypothesized "interrupted rebase or
358	substep-style rewrite" as the cause. Investigation
359	2026-05-02 showed a different cause: the bot thinks a
360	destructive `jj squash` collapsed published `0.41.1-1`,
361	`-2`, `-3` into `-4` (stripping `-4`'s description), and
362	conflicts cascaded down through every descendant. The
363	published remote was untouched and held the
364	canonical-good version of every commit.
365	
366	### Recovery
367	
368	```
369	jj bookmark set init-clone-refactor \
370	  -r init-clone-refactor@origin --allow-backwards -R .
371	```
372	
373	All 13 commits from fork to tip are unconflicted on the
374	recovered branch; `cargo test --bins` passes 331/331.
375	Three commits that had been lost locally (`0.41.1-1`,
376	`-2`, `-3`) reappeared, and `-4`'s real description
377	("user config rewrite — multi-account schema") was
378	restored. Local branch state now matches the published
379	remote exactly — no force push needed when the cycle
380	eventually resumes.
381	
382	Detailed playbook captured in
383	`notes/init-clone-refactor-conflict.md` for reuse if
384	the same shape of breakage happens again. Reusable bits
385	include: the "compare local-only chids vs remote chids"
386	diagnostic, the `heads(::A & ::B)` fork-point query, and
387	the back-up-first → verify → reset → sanity-test
388	sequence.
389	
390	### Edits
391	
392	- `notes/init-clone-refactor-conflict.md`: rewrite.
393	  Reframed from forward-looking brief to post-mortem +
394	  reusable playbook. New sections: "Status as of
395	  2026-05-02", "Root cause", "Recovery playbook",
396	  "Diagnostic technique (reusable)". Trimmed: open
397	  question on cleanup version label (moot — no commits
398	  authored, just a bookmark reset).
399	- `Cargo.toml`: bump to 0.42.0-4.6.
400	- `notes/todo.md`: `## Done` entry; reference [74]
401	  points here.
402	- `notes/chores-07.md`: this subsection.
403	
404	### Decisions made during recovery
405	
406	- **Reset bookmark vs resolve in place.** Reset wins
407	  because every local-only chid had a clean
408	  counterpart on the remote — zero unique work to
409	  preserve. Resolving 8 conflicted commits in place
410	  would have been mechanical and error-prone for no
411	  benefit.
412	- **Defer merge direction.** Whether `0.41.1` rebases
413	  onto `0.42.0` (→ `0.43.0` or `0.42.1`) vs some
414	  other topology stays open. The recovery just clears
415	  the technical-feasibility risk; the topology call
416	  is independent and can wait.
417	- **Keep the orphan local rewrites.** 8 broken
418	  local-only chids are now bookmark-less orphans in
419	  `jj log`. `jj abandon` cleanup is cosmetic;
420	  deferred.
421	
422	## --scope enum refactor (0.42.0)
423	
424	Picks up the work the 0.41.0 cycle redirected away from. The
425	foundation captured in [2] (chores-06's `### 0.41.0-4: capture
426	--scope enum vocabulary`) lands here as code: the `Scope`
427	type becomes a sum (`Roles(Vec<Side>) | Single(PathBuf)`), the
428	flag surface unifies under `--scope` / `-s` with both keyword and
429	path forms, and every dual-repo-aware command migrates to the
430	new shape in dependency order.
431	
432	**Cycle steps (initial sketch — sub-step boundaries can shift
433	once each command's call sites are seen).**
434	
435	- **0.42.0-0** — this plan + version bump + new
436	  `notes/chores-07.md`. Notes only.
437	- **0.42.0-1** — `scope.rs` enum. Internal only — no
438	  CLI changes yet, no consumers updated. Existing
439	  `Scope(Vec<Side>)` → `enum Scope { Roles(Vec<Side>),
440	  Single(PathBuf) }`. Helpers (`has_code`, `has_bot`,
441	  `is_code_only`, `is_both`, `is_empty`) shift to operate
442	  on `Roles` only; `Single(_)` is a distinct mode. Tests
443	  follow.
444	- **0.42.0-2** — custom CLI value parser for `--scope` +
445	  retrofit `init`. Parser handles the keyword set
446	  (`code|bot|code,bot|bot,code`) and the prefixed-path
447	  form (`./...`, `/...`, `~/...`). Bare names that aren't
448	  keywords error with a "did you mean `./foo`?" hint.
449	  Init is the smallest consumer (it already takes
450	  `--scope=code|bot|code,bot`); migrating it first proves
451	  the parser end-to-end before sync's more complex
452	  resolution logic comes onto the new shape.
453	- **0.42.0-3** — retrofit `sync`. Drop `-R/--repo`. Add
454	  `-s` short form. Default-scope rules update to the
455	  three-state model from [2]: dual workspace →
456	  `Roles([Code, Bot])`, single-repo workspace →
457	  `Roles([Code])`, POR → `Single(cwd)`. `scope_to_repos`
458	  in `common.rs` updates to handle the `Single(_)`
459	  variant. Update sync's integration tests where they
460	  exercise the resolver chain.
461	- **0.42.0-4** — `push --scope`. State machine becomes
462	  scope-aware: each stage either runs or no-ops based on
463	  scope. `Single(<path>)` mode means single-repo push
464	  (no `commit-claude` / `bookmark-claude` /
465	  `finalize-claude`, no `ochid:` trailer). `Roles(...)`
466	  modes work as captured in chores-06 [1]. Persist scope
467	  in `PushState` so resumes use the same scope. Add
468	  integration tests for each scope shape.
469	- **0.42.0-5** — `finalize --scope`. Replace the existing
470	  `--repo` flag with `--scope` (`Roles` for the dual case,
471	  `Single(<path>)` for the single-repo case). `--repo`
472	  goes away; per the 0.41.0-3 capture this is the
473	  intentional break, not a deprecation.
474	- **0.42.0-6** — `clone --scope`. Parallel to init —
475	  bootstrap with the appropriate scope. Single-repo clone
476	  via the path form for `vc-template-x1`-shape remotes.
477	- **0.42.0-7** — Single(_) dogfood validation. Apply the
478	  full `sync → edit → push` flow against a fresh POR or
479	  single-repo fixture (likely the `vc-template-x1` repo
480	  itself, since it's the canonical single-repo target).
481	  Surface and fix anything the unit tests miss.
482	- **0.42.0 (final)** — cycle close-out. Drops the
483	  `-N` suffix; updates `notes/todo.md`'s In Progress and
484	  Done sections; chores-07 close-out subsection.
485	
486	**Deferred to later cycles (per [2]).**
487	
488	- `validate-desc` / `fix-desc` `--scope`. Read-side
489	  commands; `Single(_)` errors there (validate compares
490	  two repos by definition).
491	- `chid` / `desc` / `list` / `show` — CommonArgs sweep.
492	  All four pick up `--scope` via a shared change to
493	  `CommonArgs`; existing `-R/--repo` retires there too.
494	- `--message-file PATH` for push. Independent push
495	  feature; gates the CLAUDE.md refresh from CLAUDE2.
496	
497	**References.** [2] points back at chores-06's
498	`### 0.41.0-4: capture --scope enum vocabulary` for
499	the full vocabulary, type-model, and per-command
500	applicability matrix. Read that subsection first before
501	diving into any of the `-N` steps below.
502	
503	### 0.42.0-1: scope.rs enum
504	
505	Internal-only refactor: tuple-struct `Scope(Vec<Side>)` →
506	`enum Scope { Roles(Vec<Side>), Single(PathBuf) }`. No CLI
507	surface changes, no consumer logic changes — every existing
508	caller still constructs and consumes `Roles(_)`; `Single(_)`
509	is staged for the parser (-2) and the sync resolver (-3).
510

531	Build is warning-clean; 257 tests pass (255 baseline + 2

534	### 0.42.0-2: --scope parser + retrofit init
535	
536	Lands the custom CLI value parser and migrates `init` onto
537	the new `Scope` shape. Parser is the shared boundary every
538	remaining `--scope` consumer (sync, push, finalize, clone)
539	will route through in -3+; init is the smallest first
540	consumer and exercises the keyword path end-to-end. The
541	path form is accepted by the parser but rejected at init
542	with a hint pointing at `--repo-local` / `--repo-remote`
543	(init creates a workspace, not a single-repo project).
544	
545	- `src/scope.rs`: `pub fn parse_scope(s: &str) -> Result<Scope, String>`.
546	  Accepts the four keyword forms (`code`, `bot`, `code,bot`,
547	  `bot,code`) plus path forms prefixed by `./`, `../`, `/`,
548	  `~/`, or the bare `~`. Anything else errors with a hint
549	  pointing at the prefix-disambiguation rule. Order is
550	  preserved on the keyword forms. Drops the now-dead
551	  `is_empty` helper (parser rejects empty input upstream;
552	  the only consumer that called it was init's redundant
553	  validation). 13 new parser unit tests.
554	- `src/init.rs`: `--scope` field flips from
555	  `Option<Vec<Side>>` (clap `value_delimiter = ','`) to
556	  `Option<Scope>` with `value_parser = crate::scope::parse_scope`.
557	  `plan_init` matches on the parsed `Scope`: `Roles(_)`
558	  takes the existing bot-only-fatal path; `Single(_)` is
559	  rejected with the init-specific hint. Test scaffolding
560	  (`fixture_scoped`) and 5 fixture call sites migrated.
561	  `scope_parses_*` tests retitled around the new field
562	  type. New `scope_path_form_rejected_at_init` test pins
563	  the rejection contract.
564	
565	Smoke-tested end-to-end via CLI:
566	
567	- `vc-x1 init tf1 --scope code --dry-run` → succeeds
568	  (single-repo dry-run plan).
569	- `vc-x1 init tf1 --scope code,bot --dry-run` → succeeds.
570	- `vc-x1 init tf1 --scope bot --dry-run` → bot-only fatal
571	  (existing `plan_init` check).
572	- `vc-x1 init tf1 --scope ./foo --dry-run` → path-form
573	  fatal (new `plan_init` check).
574	- `vc-x1 init tf1 --scope '~/work' --dry-run` → path-form
575	  fatal.
576	- `vc-x1 init tf1 --scope foo --dry-run` → clap-level
577	  parser error with hint about `./foo` disambiguation.
578	
579	### 0.42.0-3: sync --scope retrofit
580	
581	Migrates `sync` onto the new `Scope` shape and lands the
582	`Single(_)` resolver wiring. `-R/--repo` retires (single-
583	repo callers move to `--scope=<path>`); `-s` becomes the
584	short form of `--scope`. Default-scope rules pick up the
585	three-state model captured in chores-06's [1] (-4
586	vocabulary): dual workspace → `Roles([Code, Bot])`,
587	single-repo workspace → `Roles([Code])`, POR →

1	# init-clone-refactor: recovery post-mortem + playbook
2	
3	Drafted 2026-05-01 as a forward-looking brief while the
4	branch was still broken; rewritten 2026-05-02 after the
5	recovery completed. Documents what went wrong, the
6	playbook that fixed it, and the strategic decisions
7	still open.
8	
9	## Status as of 2026-05-02 (post-recovery)
10	
11	- `init-clone-refactor` bookmark is at `a8c1eefe`
12	  (chid `sumruqqomnzs`) — the published clean
13	  `0.41.1-6.5` commit.
14	- All 13 commits from fork point `ykxrlkkwqpyv`
15	  (`0.41.0`, cid `6747a27b`) through `0.41.1-6.5` are
16	  unconflicted. `cargo test --bins` passes 331/331 on
17	  the recovered tip.
18	- Recovery via `jj bookmark set init-clone-refactor
19	  -r init-clone-refactor@origin --allow-backwards
20	  -R .`. The published remote branch turned out to
21	  be the canonical-good version; local was holding a
22	  destructively-rewritten copy.
23	- 8 broken local-only chids still visible as orphans
24	  in `jj log` (no bookmark, GC-eligible). `jj abandon`
25	  cleanup is cosmetic, deferrable.

27	## Status as of 2026-05-01 (pre-recovery, historical)
28	
29	- Branch existed locally with 8 commits all in
30	  conflict state: `0.41.1-0` (chore: open) → unnamed
31	  → `0.41.1-5` (init reshape) → `0.41.1-6.0..6.5`
32	  (POR baseline tests then progressive split of
33	  `create_local_repo`). Conflicts on the same files
34	  all the way down: `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`,
35	  `notes/todo.md`, `notes/chores-08.md` (3-sided),
36	  `src/config.rs`, `src/init.rs`, `src/clone.rs`.
37	- Original brief's hypothesis — "interrupted rebase
38	  or substep-style rewrite" — turned out to be wrong.
39	  See "Root cause" below.
40	- `@origin (ahead by at least 10 commits, behind by
41	  8 commits)` was what jj reported. The "ahead by
42	  *at least* 10" caveat (vs the precise "behind by
43	  8") was the tell that the remote held more commits
44	  than local — i.e. local had *lost* work, not just
45	  rewritten it. Worth noticing earlier next time.
46	
47	## Why this matters (intent)
48	
49	Refactor `init` and `clone` to share `init_one` /
50	`clone_one` primitives plus orchestrators
51	(`init_dual` / `clone_dual`). Eliminates duplicated
52	lifecycle logic between init and clone, and gives a
53	single chokepoint for `--scope`-related changes —
54	important because every subcommand picking up
55	`--scope` lands on the same `Scope` shape, and
56	reusable foundations mean those changes happen in
57	one place rather than parallel implementations.
58	Design captured in `notes/chores-07.md > ## init +
59	clone redesign (0.41.1)`.
60	
61	## Root cause (post-mortem)
62	
63	The bot thinks the local rewrites came from a
64	destructive `jj squash` (or equivalent operation)
65	that collapsed published commits `0.41.1-1`, `-2`,
66	`-3` into `-4`. Symptoms supporting this:
67	
68	- Three chids existed on the remote (`mkwqykksykoo`
69	  `-1`, `plxsuynrtwks` `-2`, `xtrworrlkozn` `-3`)
70	  with no local counterpart at all.
71	- `uuzwzxzkszwq` (the local "unnamed" commit) shared
72	  the *same chid* as the remote `0.41.1-4` commit,
73	  but had its description stripped — consistent with
74	  a squash that pulled three commits' content into
75	  `-4`'s tree but lost the descriptions.
76	- Conflicts cascaded down `-5` and `-6.0..6.5`
77	  because those commits' parent tree no longer
78	  matched what they had been authored against — the
79	  rewritten `-4` diverged from what the descendants
80	  expected.
81	
82	Net effect: 3 commits of real work disappeared from
83	local, `-4`'s description was lost, and every
84	descendant inherited a conflict against the changed
85	parent tree. The remote was untouched and held the
86	canonical-good version of all of it.
87	
88	## Strategic decision (still deferred)
89	
90	Keep `main` + the `0.42.0-X` series as-is. Now that
91	`init-clone-refactor` is recovered, we have a clean
92	branch from which to make the merge-direction call:
93	
94	- rebase `0.41.1` on top of `0.42.0` as a `0.43.0`
95	  (or `0.42.1`), or
96	- vice versa (force-rewrite main — only if there's
97	  a reason that justifies rewriting published
98	  history),
99	- or some other topology.
100	
101	Decision still deferred. The recovery removes the
102	"is the branch even salvageable" risk; the topology
103	question is now purely about scope and review
104	ergonomics, not technical feasibility.
105	
106	## Recovery playbook (used 2026-05-02)
107	
108	Reusable for similar "local rewrites broke a branch
109	that the remote holds in clean form" situations.
110	All read-only steps come first; mutation only after
111	verification.
112	
113	1. **Back up first.** Cheap insurance.
114	   - `cp -a . ../<dir>-<date>-N` — full snapshot,
115	     includes `.jj/op` history. Or `rsync -a
116	     --exclude=target/ ./ ../<dir>/` if `target/`
117	     bloat matters.
118	   - `git clone <remote> ../<dir>-clone-<date>-N`
119	     — canonical published state, jj-free, browsable
120	     in gitk without local-rewrite confusion.
121	   - `jj op log -R . > ../<dir>-op-log-<date>-N.txt`
122	     — text-readable op log, independent of jj.
123	
124	2. **Verify remote tip is unconflicted (read-only):**
125	   ```
126	   jj git fetch -R .
127	   jj log -R . -r '<fork-chid>::<branch>@origin' \
128	     -T '... if(conflict, "CONFLICT", "ok") ...'
129	   ```
130	   Every line must read `[ok]`. If any are
131	   `[CONFLICT]`, the remote isn't a safe target and
132	   this playbook doesn't apply.
133	
134	3. **Verify local-only commits are all chid-rewrites
135	   of remote commits (read-only):**
136	   ```
137	   jj log -R . -r '<branch>@origin..<branch>' \
138	     -T 'change_id.short() ++ ...'
139	   ```
140	   Every chid here must also appear on the remote.
141	   If any chid is unique to local, the reset would
142	   lose work — investigate before proceeding.
143	
144	4. **Reset bookmark to remote:**
145	   ```
146	   jj bookmark set <branch> -r <branch>@origin \
147	     --allow-backwards -R .
148	   ```
149	   The `--allow-backwards` is required because jj
150	   guards against accidental backwards moves.
151	
152	5. **Sanity test:**
153	   ```
154	   jj new <branch> -R .
155	   cargo test --bins      # or full cargo clean ; cargo test for paranoia
156	   ```
157	
158	6. **Optional cleanup:** `jj abandon` the orphan
159	   local-only chids if you don't want them in
160	   `jj log`. Cosmetic; jj's GC eventually prunes
161	   them anyway.
162	
163	## Diagnostic technique (reusable)
164	
165	Two questions to ask when a branch is mysteriously
166	conflicted:
167	
168	- **Are the same chids on the remote in clean form?**
169	  `jj log -r '<fork>::<branch>@origin'` with the
170	  conflict-status template tells you whether the
171	  remote has a working version of every commit.
172	- **Is local missing any chids the remote has?**
173	  Compare chid lists between
174	  `<fork>::<branch>@origin` and
175	  `<fork>::<branch>` — gaps indicate commits the
176	  local branch *lost* (squash, abandon, or similar).
177	
178	If both yield "yes, remote clean + local missing
179	chids," the reset playbook above is the shortest
180	path. If only the first yields yes (no missing
181	chids, local just rewrote into broken state), you
182	can shrink step 1 of the playbook (work-loss risk
183	is gone) and just reset.
184	
185	The fork point itself can be derived without
186	hand-scanning parent columns:
187	```
188	jj log -r 'heads(::<branch-A> & ::<branch-B>)'
189	```
190	Returns the latest commit that's an ancestor of
191	both — the divergence point.
192	
193	## JJ-CONFLICT-README (the thing that shows in gitk)
194	
195	When jj has a conflicted commit, it serializes the
196	conflict state into a git-side tree containing:
197	
198	- `JJ-CONFLICT-README` — text file explaining the
199	  format.
200	- `.jjconflict-base-N/` — the base side(s) of the
201	  three-way merge.
202	- `.jjconflict-side-N/` — each conflict input.
203	
204	Plain git tools (gitk, GitHub UI, etc.) display
205	this as real files. **Recoverable** — `jj` itself
206	knows the commit is conflicted and `jj resolve`
207	walks the inputs. Don't `git checkout` a conflicted
208	commit and start editing files directly; jj's
209	invariants get confused. The README itself includes
210	the recovery hint (`jj abandon` if you accidentally
211	end up in this state).
212	
213	## Open questions
214	
215	Merge direction and chores-NN ordering moved to
216	`notes/todo.md > ## Todo` (top entries) for
217	discoverability. Remaining technical question:
218	
219	- **Conflict surface against 0.42.0.** Now that
220	  `init-clone-refactor` is clean, what's the actual
221	  diff vs current `main`? `0.42.0-1/2/3` rewrote
222	  `init.rs` / `scope.rs` / `common.rs`; `0.41.1-5/
223	  -6.x` also touched those. Real conflicts will
224	  surface only at the rebase step.
225	
226	## References
227	
228	- `notes/chores-07.md > ## init + clone redesign
229	  (0.41.1)` — full design captured in 0.42.0-4
230	  (docs-only commit on main).
231	- `notes/jj-revsets.md` — revset primitives used in
232	  the diagnostic and playbook above (`..` exclusive
233	  range, `::` inclusive DAG range, `heads()`,
234	  template `if(conflict, ...)`).
235	- `notes/substep-protocol.md` — close-out squash
236	  recipe for substep ladders. Note: this branch's
237	  `-6.0..6.5` ladder was authored before the
238	  protocol was formalized; the protocol postdates
239	  it.

I've read the recovery notes and walked the branch. Here's the picture.

**Branch state — `init-clone-refactor` is recovered and clean:**
- 12 unique commits on top of `0.41.0` (`6747a27b`): `0.41.1-0` … `0.41.1-6.5`
- Tip: `sumruqqo/a8c1eefe` "refactor: split create_local_repo lifecycle (0.41.1-6.5)"
- All commits unconflicted on the recovered branch (matches `init-clone-refactor@origin`)
- Notes claim 331/331 `cargo test --bins` on the tip
- 8 broken local-only chids still hang as orphans (`sumruqqo/0`, `orxupkxs/0`, `zstmxvrs/0` etc., all `(divergent) (conflict)`) — cosmetic, deferred

**What the branch is — design from `chores-07.md > ## init + clone redesign (0.41.1)`:**
- Refactor `init` and `clone` onto shared primitives: `init_one`/`clone_one` + orchestrators `init_dual`/`clone_dual`
- Unify their CLI: `<TARGET> [NAME] [--scope code,bot|por]`, parse-target enum (`Url | OwnerName | Path`)
- Init becomes "smart" (state-detects target); clone stays "dumb"
- Single chokepoint for `--scope` changes — important because the `0.42.0` cycle is rolling `--scope` through every command

**Ladder shipped on the branch (12 commits):**
- `-0` plan + bump · `-1` shared `repo_url` module · `-2` clone reshape + `symlink::install` · `-3` user config module · `-4` user config rewrite (multi-account) · `-5` init reshape + args module · `-6.0` POR baseline tests · `-6.1`…`-6.5` progressive split of `create_local_repo` lifecycle (substep ladder, never closed out)

**Open strategic question (per `init-clone-refactor-conflict.md > ## Strategic decision`):**
Branch was forked from `0.41.0` and never rebased. Main has since shipped `0.42.0-1/2/3` which rewrote `init.rs` / `scope.rs` / `common.rs` — the same files `-5` / `-6.x` touched. Real conflicts only surface at rebase. Three options on the table:
1. Rebase `0.41.1` onto `0.42.0` → ship as `0.43.0` or `0.42.1`
2. Force-rewrite main (only with strong justification — published history)
3. Other topology

Also outstanding: the `-6.0…6.5` substep ladder was authored before the substep protocol was formalized and was never squashed via the close-out recipe. And `chores-08.md` (per-step subsections for these commits) lives on the branch and would need to be reconciled with `chores-06/07` on main.

What angle do you want to dig into — the rebase conflict surface, the substep close-out, or the merge topology call?

> AGENT

Good news — nothing new went wrong today. The divergence you're seeing is the **pre-existing** orphan set from the 2026-05-02 recovery, exactly what the recovery notes flagged as "8 broken local-only chids still visible as orphans in `jj log` (no bookmark, GC-eligible). `jj abandon` cleanup is cosmetic, deferrable" (`notes/init-clone-refactor-conflict.md:24-25`).

**Evidence from the op log** (`jj op log`, most recent first):

```
b98e121d  jj undo                              ← undid (5e75d40d)
953b3a04  jj undo                              ← undid (e6eb0629)
5e75d40d  jj edit -r main --ignore-immutable
e6eb0629  jj edit -r init-clone-refactor       ← landed @ on a8c1eefe
b4acd1f7  jj bookmark delete wip
a2a313d7  jj edit -r wip
```

There is no `snapshot working copy` operation between `e6eb0629` and `5e75d40d`, so `a8c1eefe` was never rewritten — `jj edit` only moves `@` onto a commit; rewrites only happen if the working copy contents differ AND a subsequent command takes a snapshot. Neither happened. The two `jj undo`s rolled the operation pointer back to `b4acd1f7`. `@` is now back at `ptxlnuqs a39c903b` on top of main, working copy clean.

**The divergent chids you see** (7 of them, all `0.41.1-5` through `-6.5`):

```
sumruqqo  /a8c1eefe (clean, on init-clone-refactor)  vs  /ae61955c (orphan, conflict)
orxupkxs  /1f743351 (clean)                          vs  /b3341426 (orphan, conflict)
zstmxvrs  /68450fb9 (clean)                          vs  /5e296be3 (orphan, conflict)
znurqzvp  /90ba6ac7 (clean)                          vs  /a2c302ae (orphan, conflict)
puynnkmp  /64f59ae7 (clean)                          vs  /352fd64b (orphan, conflict)
smvxvzyp  /e847772e (clean)                          vs  /8e42f80c (orphan, conflict)
otkprmrn  /f5ec4d8b (clean)                          vs  /8e47deb2 (orphan, conflict)
```

The `/4` (clean) side of each pair came back during the bookmark reset to `init-clone-refactor@origin`. The `/0` (broken) side is the residue from the original destructive `jj squash`. They've been sitting there since 2026-05-02; today's actions didn't add any.

**Confirmation**: `init-clone-refactor` bookmark = `init-clone-refactor@origin` = `a8c1eefe` exactly. No new commits, no force-push pending, no work lost.

If the orphans bug you visually, `jj abandon <cid>` on each of the 7 broken commit IDs (`ae61955c b3341426 5e296be3 a2c302ae 352fd64b 8e42f80c 8e47deb2`) clears them. Cosmetic — they'd GC eventually.

> AGENT

Definitive answer via op-log time travel: **the divergence was present at the moment of the recovery.**

Time travel evidence (using `--at-op`):

```
=== one op BEFORE recovery (3a94c671, 2026-05-01 18:23):
sumruqqo/ae61955c   refactor: split create_local_repo lifecycle (0.41.1-6.5)
                    ← only the broken commit visible, no divergence

=== AT the recovery op (9e84fcaf, 2026-05-02 20:30, bookmark set):
sumruqqo/ae61955c   refactor: split create_local_repo lifecycle (0.41.1-6.5)
sumruqqo/a8c1eefe   refactor: split create_local_repo lifecycle (0.41.1-6.5)
                    ← both visible immediately after the bookmark moved
```

And `jj log -r su` errors with the divergent-chid message **at the recovery op** with the identical wording you'd see now:

```
=== jj log -r su --at-op 9e84fcaf9263 ===
Error: Change ID `su` is divergent
Hint: Use change offset to select single revision: su/0, su/4
```

So `jj log -r su` would have errored yesterday too, the moment after `jj bookmark set init-clone-refactor -r init-clone-refactor@origin --allow-backwards`. Today's `jj edit -r init-clone-refactor` + two `jj undo`s left the divergent set unchanged.

**Why this happens**: `jj bookmark set --allow-backwards` only *moves the bookmark*. It does not abandon the commits the bookmark used to point at. The broken ladder (`-6.0` through `-6.5`) is still a connected subgraph in jj's view because each broken commit is the parent of the next, and the head (`ae61955c`) has no living descendants — so it stays visible as an "orphan head". The recovery playbook's optional step 6 ("`jj abandon` if you don't want them in `jj log`") exists exactly because the bookmark reset doesn't clean up the old chain.

**The bot thinks** what you're remembering as "not divergent yesterday" is most likely one of:
- you ran `jj log -r init-clone-refactor` (doesn't error — just resolves the bookmark to `a8c1eefe` — though it does mark the result `(divergent)`), and didn't try the prefix form `-r su`, OR
- you saw the recovery-success message and the working `jj log` graph view, where the orphan ladder appears as a side branch you mentally treated as "old broken stuff" rather than a chid collision.

Either way, no work has been lost, no new orphan was created today, and `init-clone-refactor` still equals `init-clone-refactor@origin` exactly. If the orphans bother you, the playbook's deferred step 6 is one command per cid — or `jj abandon -r 'change_id(sumruqqo)/0 | change_id(orxupkxs)/0 | …'` to take them all in one shot.

243	
244	- `-0` — plan + version bump + `notes/forks-multi-user.md`
245	  capture + `notes/chores-08.md` (this section) +

249	  `derive_session_url` to a new `src/repo_url.rs`; stage
250	  `Target` + `parse_target` for `-2`/`-3` consumers; both
251	  `clone` and `init` migrate at their existing call sites
252	  (init.rs had verbatim duplicates worth de-duping in the
253	  same step). No behavior change.
254	- `-2` — clone reshape: `<TARGET>` + `[NAME]`
255	  positionals, add `--scope code,bot|por`, refactor into
256	  `clone_one` / `clone_dual`. Add target-exists pre-check.
257	- `-3` — user config (first cut):
258	  `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml` reader; flat
259	  `[default]` + `[github]` schema; standalone module.
260	  Superseded by `-4`'s redesign before any consumer
261	  wired in.
262	- `-4` — user config redesign: replace `-3`'s flat
263	  schema with `[account.<name>].repo.category.<cat>`
264	  multi-account / category structure, literal values,
265	  three-level resolution chain. Standalone; `-5` is the
266	  first consumer. See
267	  [`User config`](#user-config-0411-3-redesigned-in-0411-4).
268	- `-5` — init reshape: drop old flags
269	  (`--owner` / `--dir` / `--repo-local` / `--repo-remote`),
270	  add `<TARGET>` + `[NAME]`, `--account`, `--repo`,
271	  `--scope code,bot|por`. Refactor into `init_one` /
272	  `init_dual`. Existing create-from-empty operations
273	  work via the new shape.
274	- `-6` — init POR detection + upgrade paths
275	  (PorJjGit, PorGitOnly auto-bootstrap, PorWithPeerPor
276	  config-only).
277	- `-7` — `test_helpers::Fixture` migration; audit
278	  downstream callers across the test suite.
279	- final — cycle close-out: fill in Example Layout outputs,
280	  address `notes/vc-x1-init.md` cosmetic anomalies, document
281	  `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml` in `README.md` (simplest /
282	  full-one-account / full-two-account examples), drop
283	  the `-N` suffix.
284	
285	### User config (0.41.1-3, redesigned in 0.41.1-4)
286	
287	User-global config at `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml`
288	(XDG-compliant; honors `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME`). Backs init's
289	account- and repo-target resolution. No magic fallbacks:
290	missing file or missing key → predictable error pointing
291	at the exact key to set.
292	
293	`-3` shipped a flat first-cut schema (`[default]
294	repo-remote-provider`, `[github] owner`); `-4` replaces it
295	with the account/category structure below before init
296	becomes the first consumer in `-5`. Since `-3` had no
297	consumers, the rewrite is non-breaking.
298	
299	Schema:
300	
301	```toml
302	[default]
303	account = "home"      # default --account when absent
304	debug   = "trace"     # default --debug value when used without arg
305	
306	[account.home]
307	repo.default          = "remote"                       # default --repo cat when absent
308	repo.category.remote  = "git@github.com:winksaville"   # value for --repo remote (no =val)
309	repo.category.local   = "~/test-fixtures"              # value for --repo local (no =val)
310	
311	[account.work]
312	repo.default          = "remote"
313	repo.category.remote  = "git@github.com:anthropic"
314	repo.category.local   = "/work/fixtures"
315	```
316	
317	Three layers — each level has its own default-finding key:
318	
319	| CLI                       | Step 1: account                | Step 2: category                  | Step 3: value                                    |
320	|---|---|---|---|
321	| (no `--repo`/`--account`) | `[default].account`            | `[account.<a>.repo].default`      | `[account.<a>.repo.category].<cat>`              |
322	| `--account <a>`           | `<a>` (CLI)                    | `[account.<a>.repo].default`      | `[account.<a>.repo.category].<cat>`              |
323	| `--repo <cat>`            | `[default].account` (or CLI)   | `<cat>` (CLI)                     | `[account.<a>.repo.category].<cat>`              |
324	| `--repo <cat>=<val>`      | `[default].account` (or CLI)   | `<cat>` (CLI)                     | `<val>` (CLI, literal)                           |
325	
326	Values are **literal targets**, not section-name
327	pointers. For `category = "remote"`, the value is a URL
328	prefix (init appends `/<NAME>.git`); for `category =
329	"local"`, it's the parent dir for fixture bares. Built-in
330	categories `remote` and `local` are recognized; any other
331	category name errors in `-5` (forward-compat: future
332	cycles can add a `kind` field for user-defined categories).
333	
334	Resolution errors (each step has its own message):
335	
336	- Step 1 missing → `no account specified; set [default].account, use --account <name>, or write a top-level [repo] section`.
337	- Step 2 missing → `no default category for account '<a>'; set [account.<a>.repo].default or use --repo <cat>`.
338	- Step 3 missing → `no value for --repo <cat> in account '<a>'; set [account.<a>.repo.category].<cat> or use --repo <cat>=<val>`.
339	
340	#### Single-account shorthand: top-level `[repo]`
341

998	### Init-lifecycle refactor: prepare/commit split + cross_ref_ochids + init_one elimination (0.41.1-6.5)
999	
1000	Third DRY pass on the init lifecycle. Splits `create_local_repo`
1001	into `prepare_local_repo` + `commit_initial`, lifts step-6
1002	cross-reference rewriting into a named helper, eliminates
1003	`init_one` (inlined into `init_with_symlink`'s POR branch), and
1004	moves role-specific `.vc-config.toml`/`.gitignore` writing out
1005	of the lifecycle primitive into per-role helpers in `init.rs`.
1006	Suite stays at 331, all green.
1007	
1008	**`create_local_repo` → `prepare_local_repo` + `commit_initial`:**
1009	
1010	Splits the former primitive into two sequential operations:
1011	
1012	```
1013	fn prepare_local_repo(target, info_label, template, name) -> Result<()>
1014	fn commit_initial(target, info_label, ochid_strategy)     -> Result<String>
1015	```
1016	
1017	`prepare_local_repo` does mkdir + `git init` + `jj git init
1018	--colocate` + optional template copy + README rewrite. The
1019	working copy is left uncommitted so the caller can drop
1020	role-specific files into the tree. `commit_initial` then runs
1021	`jj commit` (with the appropriate `OchidStrategy` shape) and
1022	returns the new initial commit's chid.
1023	
1024	The split is what makes role-config extraction work: the caller
1025	runs prepare → write_code_config → commit, and the config files
1026	land in the initial commit. (See "Bug caught by the new
1027	substep protocol" below for what happened when the seam
1028	wasn't there.)
1029	
1030	**Role-config helpers (in `init.rs`):**
1031	
1032	```
1033	fn write_por_config(dir)     -> writes VC_CONFIG_APP_ONLY + GITIGNORE_APP_ONLY
1034	fn write_code_config(dir)    -> writes VC_CONFIG_CODE     + GITIGNORE_CODE
1035	fn write_session_config(dir) -> writes VC_CONFIG_SESSION  + GITIGNORE_SESSION
1036	```
1037	
1038	Each writes both `.vc-config.toml` and `.gitignore` for its
1039	role. They live in `init.rs` (not `repo_utils.rs`) because the
1040	constants they reference are role-specific to vc-x1's workspace
1041	layout — `repo_utils` stays role-agnostic.
1042	
1043	**`cross_ref_ochids` (in `repo_utils.rs`):**
1044	
1045	```
1046	fn cross_ref_ochids(code_dir, code_chid, session_dir, session_chid) -> Result<()>
1047	```
1048	
1049	Lifted verbatim from `create_dual`'s step-6 region: rewrite both
1050	initial commits' placeholder `ochid: /none` trailers via
1051	`jj describe @-` once each side's chid is known. Pairs with
1052	`OchidStrategy::Placeholder` from `commit_initial` (which writes
1053	the placeholder) — the two together implement the dual-mode
1054	cross-reference dance.
1055	
1056	**`init_one` eliminated:**
1057	
1058	The POR-branch wrapper inlined into `init_with_symlink`. After
1059	the configs/gitignores split out into `write_por_config` and
1060	the lifecycle split into prepare + commit, `init_one`'s body
1061	collapsed to ~25 lines of straight-line composition — no
1062	per-side abstraction left to justify a separate function.
1063	Replaced the dispatch `if is_dual { create_dual } else {
1064	init_one }` with `if is_dual { return create_dual(...); }`
1065	plus the inlined POR body.
1066	
1067	`create_dual` survives as a separate function (its body is
1068	larger and the unwrap-extract-then-orchestrate shape is a
1069	distinct unit).
1070	
1071	**`create_dual` cleanup:**
1072	
1073	Stale "Step N" inline comments dropped — the function names
1074	(`prepare_local_repo`, `cross_ref_ochids`, `push_repo`) now
1075	narrate the lifecycle on their own. Doc comment rewritten as a
1076	bulleted composition list referencing functions, not step
1077	numbers (step numbers shift if the lifecycle reorders, function
1078	names don't).
1079	
1080	The `info!()` lines inside `create_local_repo` and `push_repo`
1081	still carry `"Step N: ..."` labels — those land in -6.7 (single-
1082	word `label: body` convention).
1083	
1084	**Bug caught by the new substep protocol:**
1085	
1086	Substep (1) extracted role-config writing out of
1087	`create_local_repo` into post-call helpers. That left
1088	`.vc-config.toml`/`.gitignore` written *after* `jj commit`
1089	finalized the initial commit — i.e., they sat in the new empty
1090	working copy as uncommitted files. `push_repo`'s subsequent
1091	`git clean -xdf` would have wiped them entirely, and the
1092	`push_happy_claude_clean` integration test caught the related
1093	symptom (`.claude main` advanced because the session side had
1094	pending config files at push time).
1095	
1096	Resolved by substep (5): split the lifecycle (`prepare_local_repo`
1097	+ `commit_initial`) and reorder callers to write configs
1098	between the two. The seam now exists where role-config writing
1099	needs to land — between prepare and commit.
1100	
1101	Lesson logged for the substep protocol: run `cargo test --bins`
1102	after each substep, not only at close-out — would have caught
1103	the regression at (1) instead of (4).
1104	
1105	**Substep protocol — first use:**
1106	
1107	This cycle was the first to use the per-substep `@` workflow:
1108	each substep got its own `jj new` working copy, with the chain
1109	squashed into a single commit at close-out via
1110	`jj squash --from "@---..@" --into @---`. Wins on bisection
1111	(two `jj edit` jumps localized the (1)→(5) regression) and
1112	per-substep diff cleanliness. Frictions: missing
1113	per-substep `cargo test --bins` (above), and squash mechanics
1114	that are worth jotting in CLAUDE.md if the protocol is adopted
1115	formally.
1116	
1117	**WIP ladder:**
1118	
1119	Five substeps, squashed at close-out:
1120	
1121	1. Drop `config`/`gitignore` params from `create_local_repo`;
1122	   add `write_{por,code,session}_config` helpers in `init.rs`.
1123	2. Extract `cross_ref_ochids` into `repo_utils.rs`.
1124	3. Eliminate `init_one` — inline into `init_with_symlink`'s POR
1125	   branch.
1126	4. `create_dual` collapse — drop stale `Step N` comments,
1127	   tighten doc.
1128	5. Fix: split `create_local_repo` into `prepare_local_repo` +
1129	   `commit_initial` (regression from (1) — role-config was
1130	   landing after the initial commit instead of in it).
1131

1136	  fix = a separate cycle (likely 0.41.2). Then rebase the
1137	  in-flight 0.42.0 work on top of both.

```
602	### Extract create_repo + module reshape (0.41.1-6.2)
603	
604	First substantive DRY pass on `init_with_symlink`. Lifts steps 1-5
605	(mkdir, git/jj init, configs, optional template, initial commit)
606	into a generalized per-side primitive `create_repo` living in a new
607	`src/repo_utils.rs` module. The dual orchestrator drops from ~180
608	lines to ~110 by replacing two ~50-line per-side blocks with two
609	`create_repo` calls. Same suite (320) plus 3 new unit tests for
610	the helper itself: total 323, all green.
611	
612	**New module `src/repo_utils.rs`:**
613	
614	- `pub fn create_repo(target, info_label, config: Option<&str>,
615	  gitignore: Option<&str>, template: Option<&Path>, name,
616	  ochid_strategy) -> Result<chid>`. Per-side primitive returning
617	  the new initial commit's chid.
618	- `pub enum OchidStrategy { None, Placeholder }` — initial-commit
619	  message policy. POR uses `None` (plain "Initial commit"); dual
620	  uses `Placeholder` ("Initial commit\n\nochid: /none", rewritten
621	  in step 6 once both chids are known).
622	- 3 unit tests: `strategy_none_writes_plain_commit`,
623	  `strategy_placeholder_writes_ochid_none`,
624	  `no_config_or_gitignore_writes_neither_file`. Last one pins the
625	  new None-skips-write semantics.
626	
627	**Generalizations from -5/-6.1's inline shape:**
628	
629	- `info_label` (was `side_label`) — drops the dual-specific "side"
630	  connotation; the label is just for `info!()` narration. POR
631	  passes `"code"`; dual passes `"code"` then `"bot"`; future
632	  upgrade paths or scratch-repo callers can pass anything else.
633	- `config` and `gitignore` are `Option<&str>` (was `&str`). `None`
634	  skips that file's write. Future use cases:
635	  - PorJjGit / PorWithPeerPor upgrade paths in -6.7/-6.8 leave
636	    existing `.vc-config.toml` in place.
637	  - A hypothetical `vc-x1 scratch-repo` subcommand wanting a bare
638	    git+jj init without project-specific files.
639	
640	**Module rename `repo_url.rs` → `url.rs`:**
641	
642	The old name was misleading — the module's contents (`Target`
643	enum, `parse_target`, `derive_name`, `resolve_url`,
644	`derive_session_url`) parse and derive *URLs and target strings*,
645	not "repos". Pure string manipulation, no repo concept.
646	
647	- File renamed; jj tracks via content.
648	- `repo: &str` parameter on `derive_name` / `resolve_url` →
649	  `url: &str`. Internal references updated to match.
650	- Doc comments tightened: "repo URL" → "URL", "Resolve a repo
651	  argument" → "Resolve a target string", "session repo URL from a
652	  code repo URL" → "session URL from a code-side URL".
653	- All callers updated: `init.rs`, `clone.rs`, `main.rs` (`mod
654	  repo_url;` → `mod url;`).
655	
656	**Renames in `init.rs`:**
657	
658	- `init_dual` → `create_dual`. Symmetric with `create_repo` and
659	  signals that this is a creation-time orchestrator (not a
660	  generic init flow). `init_one` stays — we expect it to collapse
661	  into a 2-call composition by -6.4 (just `create_repo` +
662	  `finalize_repo`) and may not survive as a standalone function.
663	- `init_with_symlink`'s dispatcher updated to call `create_dual`.
664	
665	**Visibility surface (`pub(crate)`) — to support cross-module use
666	from `repo_utils`:**
667	
668	- `jj_chid` (was `fn`).
669	- `VC_CONFIG_APP_ONLY`, `VC_CONFIG_CODE`, `GITIGNORE_APP_ONLY`,
670	  `GITIGNORE_CODE` (were `const`). Used by repo_utils tests.
671	- `copy_template_recursive`, `rewrite_readme_first_line` were
```

> AGENT

`-6.6` is captured as one line in `notes/todo.md:32` and has no design subsection in `chores-08.md` yet. Three concerns in one substep, and the middle one (`symmetric .vc-config.toml schema`) is genuinely ambiguous from the description alone — I'd like your direction before drafting the chores design or touching code.

**Current `.vc-config.toml` schemas** (`src/init.rs:316-360`):

```toml
# POR (single-repo)            # Code side (dual)              # Session side (dual)
[workspace]                    [workspace]                     [workspace]
path = "/"                     path = "/"                      path = "/.claude"
                               other-repo = ".claude"          other-repo = ".."
```

POR is detected by absence of `other-repo`. Reader uses `workspace.path` (find-root walker) and `workspace.other-repo` (`src/common.rs:625-700` area).

**Questions before I draft a -6.6 design subsection:**

1. **`--config=none|<path>` flag (POR).** What's the user-facing intent? `--config=none` skips writing `.vc-config.toml` entirely (so the POR shape becomes "git+jj repo, no vc-x1 marker file at all" — matches the `Other(reason)` state in the init redesign table)? `--config=<path>` writes a custom file or copies one from a path? Or something else?

2. **"Symmetric `.vc-config.toml` schema (dual)".** Today both dual files have `path` and `other-repo`, but with side-specific values (`"/"` vs `"/.claude"`, `".claude"` vs `".."`). What does "symmetric" mean here — same field names but values differ (already true), or a structural change so both files literally contain the same content (e.g. a `[partner.code]`/`[partner.bot]` two-section schema where each side knows about both)? A worked example of the proposed shape would unblock me.

3. **"Dual-format reader for back-compat".** Reader needs to accept old `[workspace]` schema AND the new symmetric one during a transition window? Detection by which side first — try-new-then-old, or look for a key that disambiguates? Forever or until the next breaking-version bump?

4. **Substep boundaries.** My instinct is a 3-substep ladder: (a) reader change to accept both old + new (back-compat in, no producers yet), (b) new symmetric schema for dual init paths (now using the new reader), (c) `--config=none|<path>` flag for POR. That order means tests stay green at each step — old fixtures still parse, new init writes new shape, then POR gets the new flag. Sound right, or do you want a different breakdown?

Also — the stale `-6.7/-6.8` reference at `chores-08.md:635` (talking about PorJjGit / PorWithPeerPor upgrade paths, which the current `-6.6/-6.7` plan doesn't include) should probably get cleaned up while we're touching the chores file. Flagging.

> DEVELOPER

Right now in fn init at line 1184 write_por_config is written unconditionally, it needs to be conditional based on the --config option, but that flag should be implemented in such a way that it can hopefully be reused by other commands. Also, ATM we have create_dual, we need to implement create_por() just as we have create_dual.

```
1219	/// Create-from-empty orchestrator for `--scope=code,bot` (dual).
1220	///
1221	/// Composes (in order):
1222	/// - Code side: `prepare_local_repo` → `write_code_config` →
1223	///   `commit_initial` (`OchidStrategy::Placeholder`).
1224	/// - Session side: `prepare_local_repo` → `write_session_config`
1225	///   → `commit_initial` (`OchidStrategy::Placeholder`).
1226	/// - `cross_ref_ochids` — rewrite both initial commits' placeholder
1227	///   trailers once each side's chid is known.
1228	/// - `push_repo` for session side (no `clean_exclude`).
1229	/// - `push_repo` for code side with `clean_exclude = Some(".claude")`
1230	///   so the nested session repo survives the code-side clean.
1231	/// - `symlink::install` (when `create_symlink`).
1232	fn create_dual(
1233	    args: &InitArgs,
1234	    plan: &InitPlan,
1235	    templates: Option<(PathBuf, Option<PathBuf>)>,
1236	    visibility: &str,
1237	    create_symlink: bool,
1238	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
1239	    #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
1240	    let session_dir = plan.session_dir.as_ref().unwrap(); // OK: scope=code,bot ⇒ session_dir set
1241	    #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
1242	    let session_name = plan.session_name.as_ref().unwrap(); // OK: scope=code,bot ⇒ session_name set
1243	    #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
1244	    let session_url = plan.session_url.as_deref().unwrap(); // OK: scope=code,bot ⇒ session_url set
1245	
1246	    let (code_template, bot_template) = match templates.as_ref() {
1247	        Some((c, b)) => (Some(c.as_path()), b.as_deref()),
1248	        None => (None, None),
1249	    };
1250	
1251	    prepare_local_repo(&plan.project_dir, "code", code_template, &plan.name)?;
1252	    write_code_config(&plan.project_dir)?;
1253	    let code_chid = commit_initial(&plan.project_dir, "code", OchidStrategy::Placeholder)?;
1254	
1255	    prepare_local_repo(session_dir, "bot", bot_template, session_name)?;
1256	    write_session_config(session_dir)?;
1257	    let session_chid = commit_initial(session_dir, "bot", OchidStrategy::Placeholder)?;
1258	
1259	    cross_ref_ochids(&plan.project_dir, &code_chid, session_dir, &session_chid)?;
1260	
1261	    let session_chid_final = push_repo(
1262	        session_dir,
1263	        "session",
1264	        "Step 8",
1265	        None,
1266	        plan,
1267	        args,
1268	        visibility,
1269	        session_url,
1270	        plan.gh_session_slug.as_deref(),
1271	        plan.session_bare_path.as_deref(),
1272	    )?;
1273	    // `Some(".claude")` clean_exclude preserves the nested session
1274	    // repo through the code-side `git clean -xdf`.
1275	    let code_chid_final = push_repo(
1276	        &plan.project_dir,
1277	        "code",
1278	        "Step 9",
1279	        Some(".claude"),
1280	        plan,
1281	        args,
1282	        visibility,
1283	        &plan.code_url,
1284	        plan.gh_code_slug.as_deref(),
1285	        plan.code_bare_path.as_deref(),
1286	    )?;
1287	
1288	    let sl_opt = if create_symlink {
1289	        info!("Step 11: Creating Claude Code symlink...");
1290	        Some(symlink::install(&plan.project_dir)?)
1291	    } else {
1292	        info!("Step 11: (skipped — symlink disabled by caller)");
1293	        None
1294	    };
1295	
1296	    info!("");
1297	    info!("Done! Project created at {}", plan.project_dir.display());
1298	    info!(
1299	        "  Code repo:    {}  (chid={code_chid_final})",
1300	        plan.code_url
1301	    );
1302	    info!("  Session repo: {session_url}  (chid={session_chid_final})");

1304	        info!(

1306	            sl.symlink_path.display(),
1307	            sl.abs_target.display()
1308	        );
```

```
1	//! Which side(s) of a workspace a command should operate on.
2	//!
3	//! - `Side` is the clap `ValueEnum` — one of `code`, `bot`.
4	//! - `Scope` wraps the parsed list; a missing flag (None at the arg
5	//!   layer) means "caller decides", typically via workspace-state
6	//!   default resolution.
7	//! - See `notes/chores-06.md > Generalize --scope across commands
8	//!   (0.40.0)` for the full rationale and the vocabulary history.
9	
10	use clap::ValueEnum;
11	
12	/// One side of the workspace.
13	///
14	/// - `Code` — the primary (app) repo.
15	/// - `Bot` — the Claude Code session repo (typically at `.claude/`).
16	#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, ValueEnum)]
17	pub enum Side {
18	    Code,
19	    Bot,
20	}
21	
22	/// Parsed `--scope` value: the set of sides the command applies to.
23	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
24	pub struct Scope(pub Vec<Side>);
25	
26	impl Scope {
27	    /// True when the scope includes the code side.
28	    pub fn has_code(&self) -> bool {
29	        self.0.contains(&Side::Code)
30	    }
31	
32	    /// True when the scope includes the bot side.
33	    pub fn has_bot(&self) -> bool {
34	        self.0.contains(&Side::Bot)
35	    }
36	
37	    /// Exactly the code side — single-repo operation.
38	    pub fn is_code_only(&self) -> bool {
39	        self.has_code() && !self.has_bot()
40	    }
41	
42	    /// Both sides — dual-repo operation.
43	    pub fn is_both(&self) -> bool {
44	        self.has_code() && self.has_bot()
45	    }
46	}
47	
48	#[cfg(test)]
49	mod tests {
50	    use super::*;
51	
52	    #[test]
53	    fn code_only() {
54	        let s = Scope(vec![Side::Code]);
55	        assert!(s.is_code_only());
56	        assert!(!s.is_both());
57	    }
58	
59	    #[test]
60	    fn both_code_then_bot() {
61	        let s = Scope(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot]);
62	        assert!(!s.is_code_only());
63	        assert!(s.is_both());
64	    }
65	
66	    #[test]
67	    fn both_bot_then_code() {
68	        // Order doesn't matter — contains-based checks.
69	        let s = Scope(vec![Side::Bot, Side::Code]);
70	        assert!(s.is_both());
71	    }
72	}
73
```

```
1	//! Shared CLI parameter types and value parsers used across
2	//! multiple subcommands.
3	//!
4	//! - `ScopeKind` — typed value of `--scope` for init and clone
5	//!   (and any future subcommand that wants the same
6	//!   `code,bot|por` choice).
7	//! - `parse_scope_kind` — `value_parser` for the `--scope`
8	//!   field; one error wording shared across subcommands.
9	//! - `parse_repo_arg` — `value_parser` for the `--repo
10	//!   <cat>[=<val>]` field; produces `config::RepoSelector`.
11	//!
12	//! Per-subcommand `#[derive(Args)]` structs stay with their
13	//! subcommand because `#[arg(...)]` doc-comments drive
14	//! subcommand-specific `--help` text. Only the cross-cutting
15	//! types and parsers live here.
16	//!
17	//! TODO: the 0.42.0 sum-type cycle (`--scope=...|<path>` everywhere)
18	//! will likely extend `ScopeKind` with `Single(_)` / path-form
19	//! variants and pull more parsers in. See `notes/todo.md` and
20	//! `notes/chores-06.md > Generalize --scope`.
21	
22	use crate::config::RepoSelector;
23	
24	/// Typed value of `--scope` — the kinds of repo set a
25	/// subcommand can target.
26	///
27	/// - `CodeBot` (default) — dual-repo: code + `.claude` bot
28	///   session.
29	/// - `Por` — single repo (Plain Old Repo); no `.claude/`, no
30	///   `.vc-config.toml`.
31	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
32	pub enum ScopeKind {
33	    CodeBot,
34	    Por,
35	}
36	
37	/// Parse the `--scope` value into a `ScopeKind`.
38	///
39	/// - Accepts `code,bot` / `bot,code` (commutative) and `por`.
40	/// - Standalone `code` or `bot` errors — these are
41	///   config-lookup keywords; subcommands using `ScopeKind`
42	///   have no config-driven sides to look up against. Use `por`
43	///   for single-repo or `code,bot` for dual.
44	pub fn parse_scope_kind(s: &str) -> Result<ScopeKind, String> {
45	    match s {
46	        "code,bot" | "bot,code" => Ok(ScopeKind::CodeBot),
47	        "por" => Ok(ScopeKind::Por),
48	        "code" | "bot" => Err(format!(
49	            "'--scope={s}' is not a valid scope kind — use 'code,bot' (dual) or 'por' (single)"
50	        )),
51	        _ => Err(format!(
52	            "'--scope={s}' is not recognized — expected 'code,bot' or 'por'"
53	        )),
54	    }
55	}
56	
57	/// Parse the `--repo` value into a `config::RepoSelector`.
58	///
59	/// - `<cat>` → `RepoSelector { category, value: None }`.
60	/// - `<cat>=<val>` → `RepoSelector { category, value: Some(val) }`.
61	/// - Empty input, empty category, or empty value (after `=`)
62	///   errors.
63	pub fn parse_repo_arg(s: &str) -> Result<RepoSelector, String> {
64	    let s = s.trim();
65	    if s.is_empty() {
66	        return Err("--repo: value is empty".into());
67	    }
68	    match s.split_once('=') {
69	        Some((cat, val)) => {
70	            let cat = cat.trim();
71	            let val = val.trim();
72	            if cat.is_empty() {
73	                return Err(format!("--repo: missing category in '{s}'"));
74	            }
75	            if val.is_empty() {
76	                return Err(format!("--repo: empty value in '{s}'"));
77	            }
78	            Ok(RepoSelector {
79	                category: cat.to_string(),
80	                value: Some(val.to_string()),
```

> AGENT

Got it. Reusable `--config` parser lives in `src/args.rs` next to `ScopeKind`/`parse_scope_kind`. Concrete proposal for `-6.6`:

**Substep ladder (3 substeps, squashed at close-out into one `0.41.1-6.6` commit):**

1. **Extract `create_por()`** — verbatim lift of the inline POR body in `fn init` (lines 1177-1216 of `src/init.rs`) into a new sibling of `create_dual()`. Same signature shape (`args, plan, templates, visibility, create_symlink` — `create_symlink` is a no-op for POR but kept for symmetry, or dropped — see Q below). Dispatcher becomes:
   ```rust
   match args.scope {
       ScopeKind::CodeBot => create_dual(args, &plan, templates, visibility, create_symlink),
       ScopeKind::Por     => create_por(args, &plan, templates, visibility, create_symlink),
   }
   ```
   No behavior change. Doc comment mirrors `create_dual`'s composition list.

2. **Add `ConfigKind` + `parse_config_kind` to `src/args.rs`** — no consumers wired yet. New unit tests in `args.rs` covering each variant + error paths. Mirrors the existing `ScopeKind`/`parse_scope_kind` shape exactly.

3. **Wire `--config` into `InitArgs`; make `write_por_config` conditional in `create_por()`.** Threads the `ConfigKind` through and gates the call. New integration test for each variant.

**Why ladder rather than one commit**: substep 2 lands the reusable type with its own tests in isolation, so the next command that reaches for `--config` (clone, presumably) doesn't have to disentangle init plumbing from the parser. Substep 1 keeps the lift-only diff small and reviewable.

**Open questions before I draft the chores section / start substep 1:**

**Q1 — `ConfigKind` variants and semantics.** My read of `todo.md` is `--config=none|<path>` → 3-state with default:
```rust
pub enum ConfigKind {
    Default,        // no flag → write the canned VC_CONFIG_APP_ONLY
    None,           // --config=none → skip write_por_config entirely
    Path(PathBuf),  // --config=<path> → copy <path> to .vc-config.toml
}
```
Or is `Default` not a variant — the `Option<ConfigKind>` at the arg layer carries that and `ConfigKind` only has `None | Path(...)`? `ScopeKind` doesn't have a "Default" variant — the default is encoded by clap's `default_value_t`. I'd lean toward matching that: keep `ConfigKind = None | Path` and let clap's default be "use the canned content" (probably modeled as `Option<ConfigKind>` on the arg, `None` at the arg layer = canned).

Also for `Path(<path>)`: copy the file as-is, or read+validate (parse as TOML, check `[workspace]` shape) before writing? Validate-then-copy is safer; copy-as-is is simpler.

**Q2 — `--config` valid only with `--scope=por`?** Or also meaningful for `--scope=code,bot`? Today `create_dual` writes two role-specific configs unconditionally; the user mentioned only the POR case. Easiest path: error if `--config` is passed with `--scope=code,bot` for now (preflight check in `plan_init`), revisit when dual needs it.

**Q3 — Flag name shape.** `--config <VALUE>` with `none` as a magic keyword and everything else interpreted as a path? Or `--config <none|PATH>` with a custom parser (the proposed shape)? Same model as `--scope=por` — magic keyword + path-form coexist, parser disambiguates.

Once Q1/Q2/Q3 land I'll bump Cargo.toml to `0.41.1-6.6`, draft the chores-08 design subsection, and start substep 1.

Q2: Only valid on --scope=por
Q3: --config <none|Path> but it's optional on the command line so scripting | bots can be explicit and others can be lazy.

Extract `create_por()` from `fn init`'s inline POR body\n   (currently lines ~1177-1216 of `src/init.rs`), mirroring\n   `create_dual()` as a sibling creation-time orchestrator.\n2. Add a reusable `--config` flag in `src/args.rs` (next to\n   `ScopeKind` / `parse_scope_kind`) that gates POR's\n   `.vc-config.toml` write.\n\n#### create_por extraction\n\n`fn init`'s POR branch is currently inline straight-line\ncomposition. Lift verbatim into:\n\n```rust\nfn create_por(\n    args: &InitArgs,\n    plan: &InitPlan,\n    templates: Option<(PathBuf, Option<PathBuf>)>,\n    visibility: &str,\n    _create_symlink: bool,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>\n```\n\n- Signature mirrors `create_dual` for shape-symmetry. The\n  `create_symlink` parameter is unused in POR (no symlink in\n  single-repo); kept for now so the dispatcher stays trivial.\n- Composes (in order, parallel to `create_dual`'s doc list):\n  - `prepare_local_repo` → conditional config write (see\n    below) → `commit_initial` (`OchidStrategy::None`).\n  - `push_repo` for code side (no `clean_exclude`).\n  - No cross-reference, no session, no symlink.\n\nDispatcher in `fn init`:\n\n```rust\nif is_dual {\n    return create_dual(args, &plan, templates, visibility, create_symlink);\n}\nreturn create_por(args, &plan, templates, visibility, create_symlink);\n```\n\n#### --config flag (reusable)\n\nIn `src/args.rs`:\n\n```rust\npub enum ConfigKind {\n    None,\n    Path(PathBuf),\n}\n\npub fn parse_config_kind(s: &str) -> Result<ConfigKind, String>;\n```\n\nParser:\n\n- `none` (literal keyword) → `ConfigKind::None`.\n- Anything else (non-empty) → `ConfigKind::Path(s.into())`.\n- Empty input errors.\n\nNo path-prefix discipline (`./`, `~/`, etc.) — `--config` has\nonly one keyword (`none`), so any other string is unambiguously\na path.\n\n`InitArgs`:\n\n```rust\n/// Override the default `.vc-config.toml` write (POR only).\n///\n/// - Absent: write the canned `VC_CONFIG_APP_ONLY`.\n/// - `--config none`: skip writing.\n/// - `--config <path>`: copy the file at `<path>` to\n///   `.vc-config.toml`.\n#[arg(long = \"config\", value_name = \"none|PATH\",\n      value_parser = parse_config_kind)]\npub config: Option<ConfigKind>,\n```\n\nOptional on the command line so scripts and bots can be\nexplicit while interactive use stays lazy.\n\n#### Preflight checks (in `plan_init`)\n\n- `args.config.is_some()` with `--scope=code,bot` → error:\n  `--config is only valid with --scope=por`.\n- `Some(ConfigKind::Path(p))` with non-existent or unreadable\n  `p` → error pointing at the path.\n\n#### Conditional write in `create_por`\n\n```rust\nmatch args.config.as_ref() {\n    None                       => write_por_config(&plan.project_dir)?,\n    Some(ConfigKind::None)     => {} // skip\n    Some(ConfigKind::Path(p))  => copy_user_config(p, &plan.project_dir)?,\n}\n```\n\n`copy_user_config` is a small helper (impl-time call on\nlocation: `init.rs` vs `repo_utils.rs`). Copies as-is; no TOML\nparse, no schema validation. Malformed user configs surface\nlater via `find_workspace_root` / config-reader paths.\n\nOpen question for impl: `write_por_config` today writes both\n`.vc-config.toml` and `.gitignore`. The `--config` flag should\ncontrol only `.vc-config.toml`. Decide between (a) split\n`write_por_config` into `write_por_vc_config` +\n`write_por_gitignore` and call gitignore unconditionally, or\n(b) inline the `.gitignore` write in `create_por` and retire\n`write_por_config`. Lean (a) for symmetry with the\n`write_code_config` / `write_session_config` shape.\n\n#### WIP ladder (squashed at close-out)\n\nThree substeps:\n\n1. Extract `create_por()` — verbatim lift; no behavior change.\n2. Add `ConfigKind` + `parse_config_kind` to `src/args.rs`\n   with unit tests; no consumers wired.\n3. Wire `--config` into `InitArgs`; conditional write in\n   `create_por`; preflight + integration tests.\n\n#### Decisions made during design\n\n- **`Default` not a `ConfigKind` variant.** Absence-of-flag is\n  encoded at the `Option<ConfigKind>` arg layer (matches\n  `ScopeKind`'s shape — clap's `Option` handles \"no value\", not\n  an enum variant).\n- **`--config` only valid with `--scope=por` for now.** Dual\n  mode's per-side configs (`write_code_config` /\n  `write_session_config`) are out of scope for -6.6; revisit\n  when a use case surfaces.\n- **Path validation: existence + readable, no TOML parse.**\n  Trust user-provided files; downstream readers surface\n  malformed content. Preflight stays cheap.\n- **`Path(_)` accepts any non-`none` string.** Single keyword,\n  no `./` discipline needed.\n- **`create_symlink` parameter retained on `create_por`.** Kept\n  in the signature for shape-symmetry with `create_dual`;\n  ignored in the body. Drop in a follow-up if it accumulates\n  warnings.\n\n### Decisions made during design"
```json
}
```
